(B.6)

where each
\mathcal{L}(u)
operator acts in some quantum space (shown by vertical sites) and one auxiliary space (shown horizontally) and we denoted
a_{i}^{(1)}
as
a_{i}
, and
a_{i}^{(2)}
as
b_{i}
.

Also it will be convenient to write
\ket{\varnothing}
instead of
\ket{0}\ket{0}
.

Now let us sandwich the RLL-equation (
4.26
) between two states in the auxiliary spaces
\bra{\boldsymbol{k}}\bra{\boldsymbol{l}}\dots\ket{\boldsymbol{m}}\ket{\boldsymbol{n}}
. It can be expressed in the following way

u\rightarrow\bra{k}
v\rightarrow\bra{l}
\ket{m}
\ket{n}
=
\bra{k}
\bra{l}
\ket{m}
\ket{n}

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\Longleftrightarrow\bra{0}\bra{0}\mathcal{R}(u-v)\mathcal{L}(u)\mathcal{L}(v)\ket{0}\ket{1}=\bra{0}\bra{0}\mathcal{L}(v)\mathcal{L}(u)\mathcal{R}(u-v)\ket{0}\ket{1}

(B.12)

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}(u-v)\ket{u,u}\ket{v+1,v-1}=\mathcal{R}e^{i(\phi_{1}^{(2)}-\phi_{2}^{(2)})}\ket{\varnothing}=\Big\{\omega\overset{\text{def}}{=}(\phi_{1}^{(1)}+\phi_{1}^{(2)})-(\phi_{2}^{(1)}+\phi_{2}^{(2)})\Big\}=\\
=\mathcal{R}e^{i\omega/2}e^{-i\varphi(0)}\ket{\varnothing}=e^{i\omega/2}\mathcal{R}\frac{1}{2}\Big(\chi_{-\frac{1}{2}}-i\sqrt{2}\Psi_{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\ket{\varnothing}=e^{i\omega/2}\frac{1}{2}\Big(\chi_{-\frac{1}{2}}-i\sqrt{2}\frac{2(u-v)+iQ}{2(u-v)-iQ}\Psi_{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\ket{\varnothing}=\\
=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\ket{1}\ket{0}+\ket{0}\ket{1}-i\sqrt{2}\frac{2(u-v)+iQ}{2(u-v)-iQ}(\ket{1}\ket{0}-\ket{0}\ket{1})\Big)=\frac{\gamma}{\Delta+\gamma}\ket{1}\ket{0}+\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+\gamma}\ket{0}\ket{1}\end{split}

(B.13)

\Longrightarrow(\Delta+\gamma)h_{1}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(v)=\gamma h_{1}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u)+\Delta\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(v)h_{1}(u)

(B.14)

(B.17)

By multiplying (
B.14
) by
h_{1}^{-1}(v)
from the left and using (
B.9
) one get the first relation in (
4.33
). In order to get first one from (
4.34
) we need to multiply (
B.16
) by same factor from the right. The others relations are produced by the same method.

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\par
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\Longleftrightarrow\small{\bra{\frac{1}{2}}\bra{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathcal{R}(u-v)\mathcal{L}(u)\mathcal{L}(v)\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}=\bra{\frac{1}{2}}\bra{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}(v)\mathcal{L}(u)\mathcal{R}(u-v)\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}

(B.18)

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}\ket{u+\frac{1}{2},u-\frac{1}{2}}\ket{v-\frac{1}{2},v+\frac{1}{2}}=\mathcal{R}e^{i\varphi(0)}\ket{\varnothing}=\frac{1}{2}\Big(\chi_{-\frac{1}{2}}+i\sqrt{2}\mathcal{R}\Psi_{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\ket{\varnothing}=\\
=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}+\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}\ket{\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{2\Delta+iQ}{2\Delta-iQ}\Big(\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}-\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\Big)\bigg)=\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+\gamma}\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}+\frac{\gamma}{\Delta+\gamma}\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\end{split}

(B.19)

\Longrightarrow h_{2}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\bullet,\blacksquare}(v)=\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+\gamma}{L}_{\bullet,\blacksquare}(v)h_{2}(u)+\frac{\gamma}{\Delta+\gamma}h_{2}(v){L}_{\bullet,\blacksquare}(u)

(B.20)

Again, multiplying by
h_{2}(v)^{-1}
from the left we get the second equation from (
4.33
). The remaining equations can be obtained in the same way as for the white states.

ee and ff relations.

From RLL-equation it is obvious that

\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(v)=\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u).

(B.21)

After multiplying this by
h_{1}(u)^{-1}h_{1}(v)^{-1}
from the left we get

h_{1}^{-1}(v)e_{1}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(v)=h_{1}^{-1}(u)e_{1}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u).

(B.22)

Then we multiply the first equation in (
4.33
) by
h_{1}^{-1}(u)
from left and right

h_{1}^{-1}(u)e_{1}(v)=\frac{\Delta+\gamma}{\Delta}e_{1}(v)h_{1}^{-1}(u)-\frac{\gamma}{\Delta}e_{1}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u),

(B.23)

exchange
u
and
v
, submit it into (
B.22
) and get (
4.35
) for
i=1
. Just because all relations for
i=2
are the same, one can get similar result for
i=2
. Similarly

\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(v)=\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(u)\quad\text{and}\quad f_{1}(v)h_{1}^{-1}(u)=\frac{\Delta+\gamma}{\Delta}h_{1}^{-1}(u)f_{1}(v)-\frac{\gamma}{\Delta}h_{1}^{-1}(u)f_{1}(u)\Longrightarrow\eqref{ffii}.

(B.24)

Now let us consider another, more interesting relation between
e_{1}
and
e_{2}
. Since the
R-
matrix preserves the colour of the state, we will need only this three
RLL
-relations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.8}{0.0}{0.0}{0.8}{86.09332pt}{9.38092pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\Leftrightarrow\small{\bra{0}\bra{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathcal{R}(u-v)\mathcal{L}(u)\mathcal{L}(v)b_{-1}^{(1)}\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\ket{1}=\bra{0}\bra{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{L}(v)\mathcal{L}(u)\mathcal{R}(u-v)b_{-1}^{(1)}\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\ket{1}

(B.27)

To go further we need to know how the
R
-matrix acts on the state
\ket{\frac{1}{2}}\ket{1}
. Consider the states

\ket{\RIGHTcircle}\overset{\text{def}}{=}\ket{u,u}\ket{v+1/2,v-1/2}=e^{\frac{i}{2}(\phi_{1}^{(2)}-\phi_{2}^{(2)})}\ket{\varnothing},\quad\ket{\LEFTcircle}\overset{\text{def}}{=}\ket{u+1/2,u-1/2}\ket{v,v}=e^{\frac{i}{2}(\phi_{1}^{(1)}-\phi_{2}^{(1)})}\ket{\varnothing}

(B.28)

and their combination

(\ket{\LEFTcircle}\pm\ket{\RIGHTcircle)}=e^{i\omega/4}(e^{i\varphi/2}\pm e^{-i\varphi/2})\ket{\varnothing}\equiv e^{i\omega/4}\ket{\pm}.

(B.29)

One can find some ”vacuum” properties of the state
\ket{\pm}

\begin{split}\psi_{0}\ket{\pm}=\pm\frac{1}{i\sqrt{2}}\ket{\mp},\quad\psi_{k}\ket{\pm}=0\quad\text{for all k$>$0}\end{split}

(B.30)

This makes it invisible for R-matrix, because all fermions in R-matrix expansion are normal-ordered.
Thus we find, that
\mathcal{R}\ket{1/2}\ket{0}=\ket{1/2}\ket{0}
.

Next we consider the state
\ket{-1/2}\ket{0}

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}\ket{1}=\mathcal{R}e^{\frac{i\omega}{4}}\ket{-3/4}\ket{3/4}=\mathcal{R}e^{\frac{i\omega}{4}}e^{-\frac{i3}{2}\phi}\ket{\RIGHTcircle}=\frac{1}{2}\big(\chi_{-1}-i\sqrt{2}\mathcal{R}\psi_{-1}\big)\ket{\RIGHTcircle}\end{split}

(B.31)

Writing (
2.34
) on the first level, one has

\begin{split}G^{\pm}_{-1}=a_{-1}\psi_{0}+(a_{0}\mp iQ)\psi_{-1}+\dots,\quad\mathcal{R}G^{+}_{-1}=G^{-}_{-1}\mathcal{R}\\
\Rightarrow\pm\frac{1}{i\sqrt{2}}\mathcal{R}a_{-1}\ket{\mp}+(u-iQ)\mathcal{R}\psi_{-1}\ket{\pm}=\pm\frac{1}{i\sqrt{2}}a_{-1}\ket{\mp}+(u+iQ)\mathcal{R}\psi_{-1}\ket{\pm}\end{split}

(B.32)

and

\begin{split}L^{\pm}_{-1}=\frac{1}{2}\psi_{-1}\psi_{0}+(u\mp\frac{iQ}{2})a_{-1}+\dots,\quad\mathcal{R}L^{+}_{-1}=L^{-}_{-1}\mathcal{R}\\
\Rightarrow\pm\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{i\sqrt{2}}\mathcal{R}\psi_{-1}\ket{\mp}+(u-\frac{iQ}{2})\mathcal{R}a_{-1}\ket{\pm}=\pm\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{i\sqrt{2}}\psi_{-1}\ket{\mp}+(u+\frac{iQ}{2})a_{-1}\ket{\pm}\end{split}

(B.33)

where
a_{i}
are modes of
\Phi
. Solving this system of
4
equations one can find
\mathcal{R}\psi_{-1}\ket{\pm}
and
\mathcal{R}a_{-1}\ket{\pm}
, and thus
\mathcal{R}\psi_{-1}\ket{\RIGHTcircle}
and
\mathcal{R}a_{-1}\ket{\LEFTcircle}
.

Finally one can get

\mathcal{R}\ket{1}\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}=(...)\psi_{-1}\ket{\RIGHTcircle}+(...)a_{-1}\ket{\LEFTcircle}=(...)\ket{-\frac{1}{2}}\ket{1}+\Big((...)a_{-1}^{(1)}+(...)a_{-1}^{(2)}+(...)b_{-1}^{(1)}+(...)b_{-1}^{(2)}\Big)\ket{\LEFTcircle},

(B.34)

where by
\dots
we have hidden some explicit expressions coming from the
R-
matrix elements. In (
B.34
) the first term in the r.h.s. corresponds to exchange term, while the terms
a_{-1}^{(1)}
and
b_{-1}^{(1)}
correspond to local terms. We can get rid of the others by finding special linear combinations of (
B.26
) and (
B.27
). The
R
-matrix action in these cases can be found after solving a system of equations for
\phi_{-1}
,
a_{-1}
,
\phi_{1}^{(1)}+\phi_{1}^{(2)}
and
\phi_{2}^{(1)}+\phi_{2}^{(2)}
.
Then one finds a relation:

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\bullet,\blacksquare}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(v)+s(\Delta)\mathcal{L}_{\bullet,\bullet}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\includegraphics[scale]{horiswh.eps}}(v)+t(\Delta)\mathcal{L}_{\bullet,\bullet}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\includegraphics[scale]{vertwh.eps}}(v)=\\
=\alpha(\Delta)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\bullet,\blacksquare}(u)+\beta(\Delta)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\circ}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\bullet,\includegraphics[scale]{horisbl.eps}}(u)+\gamma(\Delta)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\circ}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\bullet,\includegraphics[scale]{vertbl.eps}}(u)\end{split}

(B.35)

where
s(\Delta)
,
t(\Delta)
,
\alpha(\Delta)
,
\beta(\Delta)
,
\gamma(\Delta)
are some coefficients.
Multiplying by
h_{1}^{-1}(v)h_{2}^{-1}(u)
from the left and get the
e_{1}e_{2}
commutation relation, after rewriting it in terms of higher currents ones gets (
4.37
). Similarly one finds (
4.38
)

ef relation.

First we use obvious relation

\big[\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\blacksquare,\bullet}(v)\big]=\big[\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\blacksquare,\bullet}(v)\big]=0.

(B.36)

Multiplying it by suitable
h_{i}^{-1}
from left and right we get (
4.39
) for i
\neq
j.

For partitions of same colors one has to consider

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.8}{0.0}{0.0}{0.8}{100.8903pt}{12.22638pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\par\par
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\Longleftrightarrow\bra{1}\bra{0}\mathcal{R}(u-v)\mathcal{L}(u)\mathcal{L}(v)\ket{1}\ket{0}=\bra{1}\bra{0}\mathcal{L}(v)\mathcal{L}(u)\mathcal{R}(u-v)\ket{1}\ket{0}

(B.37)

Then, using same steps as in (
B.13
) one finds

\mathcal{R}(u-v)\ket{1}\ket{0}=\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+\gamma}\ket{1}\ket{0}+\frac{\gamma}{\Delta+\gamma}\ket{0}\ket{1}.

(B.38)

And then, after substitution into (
B.37
) and using (
B.13
)

\begin{split}\frac{\gamma}{\Delta+\gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\square,\square}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\circ}(v)+\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+\gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(v)=\frac{\gamma}{\Delta+\gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\square,\square}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\circ}(u)+\frac{\Delta}{\Delta+\gamma}\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u)\end{split}

(B.39)

Now, multiplying (
B.39
) by
h_{1}^{-1}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(v)
from the right, using (
B.15
) and (
B.17
), and noticing that

e_{1}(u+\gamma)=\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)

(B.40)

one obtains

\begin{split}e_{1}(u+\gamma)f_{1}(v)+\frac{\gamma}{\Delta+\gamma}\Big(\mathcal{L}_{\square,\square}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)-\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)\Big)=\\
=f_{1}(v)e_{1}(u+\gamma)+\frac{\gamma}{\Delta+\gamma}\Big(\mathcal{L}_{\square,\square}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)-\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(v)h_{1}^{-1}(v)\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(v)h_{1}^{-1}(v)\Big)\end{split}

(B.41)

After shifting
u\rightarrow u-Q
and introducing

\psi(u+Q)\overset{\text{def}}{=}\mathcal{L}_{\square,\square}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)-\mathcal{L}_{\circ,\square}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)\mathcal{L}_{\square,\circ}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)

(B.42)

we get (
4.39
). Finally, just because all the eh and ef relations for R sector are identical to NS case, we come to (
4.39
) for black diagrams.

Commutation relations with
\psi
.

It is important to derive commutation relations between the original currents
h_{i}
,
e_{i}
,
f_{1}
and auxiliary current
\psi_{j}
. Formula (
4.41
) follows immediately from the results of section
5
, while (
4.43
) can be proved via definition (
4.40
) and relations (
4.37
)-(
4.38
).

Let us derive (
4.42
). Consider the relation (
4.39
) and multiply it by
e_{1}(z)
from the left

e_{1}(z)e_{1}(u)f_{1}(v)-e_{1}(z)f_{1}(v)e_{1}(u)=-e_{1}(z)\gamma\frac{\psi_{1}(u)-\psi_{1}(v)}{u-v},

(B.43)

from from the right

e_{1}(u)f_{1}(v)e_{1}(z)-f_{1}(v)e_{1}(u)e_{1}(z)=-\gamma\frac{\psi_{1}(u)-\psi_{1}(v)}{u-v}e_{1}(z),

(B.44)

Now we carry through
e_{1}(z)
to the left in (
B.44
) and subtract from the result equation (
B.43
)

\begin{split}-\frac{\gamma}{\Delta-\gamma}\big([e_{1}(z)e_{1}(z),f(v)]+[e_{1}(u)e_{1}(u),f(v)])+e_{1}(u)\frac{\psi_{1}(z)-\psi_{1}(v)}{z-v}-\\
-\Lambda(u-z)\frac{\psi_{1}(z)-\psi_{1}(v)}{z-v}e_{1}(u)=e_{1}(z)\Lambda(u-z)\frac{\psi_{1}(u)-\psi_{1}(v)}{u-v}-\frac{\psi_{1}(u)-\psi_{1}(v)}{u-v}e_{1}(z)\end{split}

(B.45)

where

\Lambda(u-z)=\frac{\gamma+\Delta}{-\gamma+\Delta},\quad\Delta=u-z.

(B.46)

In the limit
v\rightarrow\infty
this expression becomes

\begin{split}-e_{1}(u)\big(\psi_{1}(z)-C\big)+\Lambda(u-z)\big(\psi_{1}(z)-C\big)e_{1}(u)-\\
-\frac{\gamma}{\Delta-\gamma}\big([e_{1}(z)e_{1}(z),f_{1}(v)]+[e_{1}(u)e_{1}(u),f_{1}(v)])=-\Lambda(u-z)e_{1}(z)\big(\psi_{1}(u)-C\big)+\big(\psi_{1}(u)-C\big)e_{1}(z),\end{split}

(B.47)

where above we used

\psi(z)=C+\frac{\psi_{1}}{z}+...,\quad f(z)=\frac{f_{1}}{z}+....

(B.48)

Now we divide by
z-v
and substract the resulting equation from (
B.45
). Then we obtain

\begin{split}-\frac{\gamma}{\Delta-\gamma}\big([e_{1}(z)e_{1}(z),f(v)]+[e_{1}(u)e_{1}(u),f(v)])+e_{1}(u)\frac{C-\psi_{1}(v)}{z-v}-\Lambda(u-z)\frac{C-\psi_{1}(v)}{z-v}e_{1}(u)=\\
=\Lambda(u-z)e_{1}(z)\psi_{1}(u){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bigg(\frac{1}{u-v}-\frac{1}{z-v}\bigg)}+\Lambda(u-z)e_{1}(z)\bigg(\frac{C}{z-v}-\frac{\psi_{1}(v)}{u-v}\bigg)-\\
-\psi_{1}(u)e_{1}(z){\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\bigg(\frac{1}{u-v}-\frac{1}{z-v}\bigg)}-\bigg(\frac{C}{z-v}-\frac{\psi_{1}(v)}{u-v}\bigg)e_{1}(z)\end{split}

(B.49)

Now it is easy to see, that the blue factors have order
2
in large
v
expansion, while the rest terms after expansion are local. This is exactly what we wanted to get. Similarly, we derive commutation relations of
\psi_{1}
with
f_{1}
.

Appendix C
Ad representation for
\mathcal{L}_{\psi,\varnothing}(u)

In this section we will prove that for every state
|\psi\rangle\in V_{0}
with energy
N_{1}
and charge
N_{1}-N_{2}
the operator

\mathcal{L}_{\psi,\circ}(u)=\langle\psi|\mathcal{R}_{01}(u-u_{1})...\mathcal{R}_{0n}(u-u_{n})|0\rangle

(C.1)

in the Yang-Baxter algebra can be written in the form

\mathcal{L}_{\psi,\circ}(u)=\oint F_{\psi}(\mathbf{z},\mathbf{w})f_{1}(z_{1})\dots f_{1}(z_{N_{1}})f_{2}(w_{1})\dots f_{2}(w_{N_{2}})h_{1}(u)d\mathbf{z}d\mathbf{w},

(C.2)

where
F_{\psi}(\boldsymbol{z},\boldsymbol{w})
is some rational function and the contour goes around
\infty
including some points (poles of
F_{\psi}
in fact).

We introduce following notations for boson modes

i\partial\Phi_{j}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\Phi_{j}^{(k)}z^{-k-1},\quad i\partial\varphi_{j}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\varphi_{j}^{(k)}z^{-k-1},

(C.3)

where
\Phi_{j}=\phi_{1}^{(j)}+\phi_{2}^{(j)}
and
\varphi_{j}=\phi_{1}^{(j)}-\phi_{2}^{(j)}
. By definition (
4.6
)
\mathcal{R}_{ij}
commutes with
\Phi_{i}+\Phi_{j}
and
\varphi_{i}+\varphi_{j}
. Thus for
\mathcal{L}
-operator we have the following commutation relations

(\Phi_{0}^{(k)}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Phi_{j}^{(k)})\mathcal{L}(u)=\mathcal{L}(u)(\Phi_{0}^{(k)}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Phi_{j}^{(k)}),\quad\forall k\in{\mathcal{Z}}

(C.4)

(\varphi_{0}^{(k)}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}^{(k)})\mathcal{L}(u)=\mathcal{L}(u)(\varphi_{0}^{(k)}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}^{(k)}),\quad\forall k\in{\mathcal{Z}}

(C.5)

Summing them up, sandwiching the resulting equation with
\langle u+m,u-m|a_{\boldsymbol{\lambda}}b_{\boldsymbol{\mu}}
bra-vector,
|u,u\rangle
ket-vector and taking
k>0
, we obtain

\mathcal{L}_{m,\boldsymbol{\lambda}+k,\boldsymbol{\mu};\circ}=[\mathcal{L}_{m,\boldsymbol{\lambda},\boldsymbol{\mu};\circ},\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\Phi_{j}^{(k)}+\varphi_{j}^{(k)})].

(C.6)

Similarly one can derive

\mathcal{L}_{m,\boldsymbol{\lambda},\boldsymbol{\mu}+k;\circ}=[\mathcal{L}_{m,\boldsymbol{\lambda},\boldsymbol{\mu};\circ},\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\Phi_{j}^{(k)}-\varphi_{j}^{(k)})].

(C.7)

Also
R_{ij}
commutes with
e^{-i\varphi_{i}}+e^{-i\varphi_{j}}
. Then one can write

\left((e^{-i\varphi_{0}})_{k}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}(e^{-i\varphi_{j}})_{k}\right)\mathcal{L}(u)=\mathcal{L}(u)\left((e^{-i\varphi_{0}})_{k}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}(e^{-i\varphi_{j}})_{k}\right),

(C.8)

where in the last line we used the mode expansion
e^{-i\varphi}=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(e^{-i\varphi})_{k}z^{-k-1}
. Then sandwiching it with
\langle u+m,u-m|
bra-vector,
|u,u\rangle
ket-vector and taking
k=2m+1>0
, one can get

\mathcal{L}_{m+1,\varnothing,\varnothing;\circ}=[\mathcal{L}_{m,\varnothing,\varnothing;\circ},\sum_{j=1}^{n}(e^{-i\varphi_{j}})_{2m+1}],

(C.9)

and similarly

\mathcal{L}_{-m-1,\varnothing,\varnothing;\circ}=[\mathcal{L}_{-m,\varnothing,\varnothing;\circ},\sum_{j=1}^{n}(e^{i\varphi_{j}})_{2m+1}].

(C.10)

Now we will prove that the operators in the right hand side of (
C.6
), (
C.7
), (
C.9
) and (
C.10
) can be expressed in terms of
f_{1}(u)
and
f_{2}(u)
’s modes

f_{1}(u)=\frac{f_{1}^{(1)}}{u}+\frac{f_{1}^{(2)}}{u^{2}}+\dots\quad f_{2}(u)=\frac{f_{2}^{(1)}}{u}+\frac{f_{2}^{(2)}}{u^{2}}+\dots

(C.11)

It will allow us to express
\mathcal{L}_{\psi,\varnothing}
through
f_{1}(u)
,
f_{2}(u)
and
h_{1}(u)=\mathcal{L}_{\varnothing,\varnothing}
.

To obtain
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Phi_{j}^{(k)}
and
\sum_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}^{(k)}
from
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
, firstly it is useful to express
f_{\Phi}(u)\overset{\text{def}}{=}\mathcal{L}_{\Phi,\varnothing}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)
and
f_{\varphi}(u)\overset{\text{def}}{=}\mathcal{L}_{\varphi,\varnothing}(u)h_{1}^{-1}(u)
in terms of
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
. For this we need
f_{1}f_{2}
Yangian commutation relation

\displaystyle(\Delta+\alpha)(\Delta+\beta)f_{2}(u)f_{1}(v)-(\Delta+\beta)f_{\includegraphics[scale]{horisbl.eps}}(u)-(\Delta+\alpha)f_{\includegraphics[scale]{vertbl.eps}}(u)=

(C.12)

\displaystyle=(\Delta-\alpha)(\Delta-\beta)f_{1}(v)f_{2}(u)-(\Delta-\beta)f_{\includegraphics[scale]{horiswh.eps}}(v)-(\Delta-\alpha)f_{\includegraphics[scale]{vertwh.eps}}(v),

where
\Delta=u-v
and currents in the local terms are defined in (
4.50
), (
4.51
), (
4.52
) and (
4.53
). One can expand the l.h.s. and the r.h.s. of this relation at
u\rightarrow\infty
and obtain the following formulae

\displaystyle f_{\includegraphics[scale]{horiswh.eps}}(v)=\frac{1}{\beta-\alpha}\left((-2v+\beta)[f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}(v)]-\gamma\{f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}(v)\}+[f_{2}^{(2)},f_{1}(v)]+[f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}^{(1)}]\right)

(C.13)

\displaystyle f_{\includegraphics[scale]{vertwh.eps}}(v)=\frac{1}{\alpha-\beta}\left((-2v+\alpha)[f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}(v)]-\gamma\{f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}(v)\}+[f_{2}^{(2)},f_{1}(v)]+[f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}^{(1)}]\right).

At the same one has

f_{\includegraphics[scale]{horiswh.eps}}(v)=\frac{\alpha+\beta}{\alpha-\beta}(\alpha f_{\varphi}(v)-\frac{1}{2}f_{\Phi}(v)),\quad f_{\includegraphics[scale]{vertwh.eps}}(v)=\frac{\alpha+\beta}{\alpha-\beta}(\frac{1}{2}f_{\Phi}(v)-\beta f_{\varphi}(v))

(C.14)

and hence find

\displaystyle f_{\Phi}(v)=\frac{2}{\alpha+\beta}\left((-2v-\gamma)[f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}(v)]-\gamma\{f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}(v)\}+[f_{2}^{(2)},f_{1}(v)]+[f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}^{(1)}]\right),

(C.15)

\displaystyle f_{\varphi}(v)=\frac{1}{\alpha+\beta}[f_{2}^{(1)},f_{1}(v)].

Considering the large
u
expansion of the
R
-matrix, one can write

\displaystyle f_{\Phi}^{(1)}=\gamma\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Phi_{j}^{(1)},\quad f_{\varphi}^{(1)}=2\gamma\sum_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}^{(1)},

(C.16)

\displaystyle f_{\Phi}^{(2)}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\gamma(u_{j}-(j-1)\gamma)\Phi_{j}^{(1)}+\frac{\gamma}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum\limits_{l\neq 0,1}\Phi_{j}^{(1-l)}\Phi_{j}^{(l)}+\gamma\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{l\geq 0}\varphi_{j}^{(1-l)}\varphi_{j}^{(l)},

which allows to express

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Phi_{j}^{(k)}=\frac{(-1)^{k-1}}{\gamma^{k}(k-1)!}\text{ad}^{k-1}_{f_{\Phi}^{(2)}}(f_{\Phi}^{(1)}),

(C.17)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\varphi_{j}^{(k)}=\frac{(-1)^{k-1}}{(2\gamma)^{k}(k-1)!}\text{ad}^{k-1}_{f_{\Phi}^{(2)}}(f_{\varphi}^{(1)}).

Using
4.57
one can rewrite these formulas through
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
modes.

Similarly, for
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
modes one has

f_{1}^{(1)}=\gamma\sum_{j=1}^{n}(e^{-i\varphi_{j}})_{1},\quad f_{2}^{(1)}=\gamma\sum_{j=1}^{(n)}(e^{i\varphi_{j}})_{0}.

(C.18)

And then using the commutation relations

[\varphi_{j}^{(k)},(e^{i\varphi_{j}})_{n}]=2(e^{i\varphi_{j}})_{n+k},\quad[\varphi_{j}^{(k)},(e^{-i\varphi_{j}})_{n}]=-2(e^{-i\varphi_{j}})_{n+k},

(C.19)

one can express any mode of
\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{i\varphi_{j}}
and
\sum_{j=1}^{n}e^{-i\varphi_{j}}
fields through
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
modes. So, we have proved that any operator of type
\mathcal{L}_{\psi,\varnothing}
can be constructed from
h_{1}(u)
via some commutations with
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
modes.

To understand that integral representation
C.2
occurs, let us consider an example.

According to our general scheme, one can express
\mathcal{L}_{1,\varnothing}
through
h_{1}(u)
using (
C.9
)

\mathcal{L}_{1,\varnothing,\varnothing;\circ}(u)=[h_{1}(u),\sum_{j=1}^{n}(e^{-i\varphi_{j}})_{1}].

(C.20)

Then the expression (
C.18
) should be used. So, one can write

\gamma\mathcal{L}_{1,\varnothing,\varnothing;\circ}(u)=[h_{1}(u),f_{1}^{(1)}].

(C.21)

With the integral expansion for
f_{1}^{(1)}
the last formula can be rewritten as

\gamma\mathcal{L}_{1,\varnothing,\varnothing;\circ}(u)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\infty}(h_{1}(u)f_{1}(z)-f_{1}(z)h_{1}(u))=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\infty+{u}}(h_{1}(u)f_{1}(z)-f_{1}(z)h_{1}(u)),

(C.22)

where in the last equality the contour is deformed in such way, that point
u
is inside the contour. Then one can substitute

h_{1}(u)f_{1}(z)=\frac{u-z+\gamma}{u-z}f_{1}(z)h_{1}(u)-\frac{\gamma}{u-z}\mathcal{L}_{1,\varnothing}(u)

(C.23)

and forget about local term inside the integral. Finally one can get

\mathcal{L}_{1,\varnothing,\varnothing;\circ}(u)=-\frac{1}{2\pi i}\oint_{\infty+u}\frac{f_{1}(z)h_{1}(u)}{z-u}dz.

(C.24)

It is similar to (
C.2
) with
F_{1}(z)=-\frac{1}{2\pi i(z-u)}
. Applying such trick with contour deformations in the way that local terms do not appear, one can receive an integral representation for any
\mathcal{L}_{\psi,\varnothing}
operator.
